The **Pfizer–BioNTech COVID-19 vaccine**, sold under the brand name **Comirnaty**,[^cite_note-Comirnaty_EPAR-2][^cite_note-FDA_PR_20210823-37] is an [mRNA-based](63136688.md) [COVID-19 vaccine](63319438.md) developed by the German biotechnology company [BioNTech](64671486.md). For its development, BioNTech collaborated with the American company [Pfizer](62304.md) to carry out [clinical trials](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clinical_trial), logistics, and manufacturing.[^cite_note-38][^cite_note-nytimes-2020-11-09-39] It is authorized for use in humans to provide protection against [COVID-19](63030231.md), caused by infection with the [SARS-CoV-2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Severe_acute_respiratory_syndrome_coronavirus_2) [virus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virus).[^cite_note-Comirnaty_EPAR-2][^cite_note-Pfizer-BioNTech_COVID-19_Vaccine_FDA_label-40] The vaccine is given by [intramuscular injection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intramuscular_injection).[^cite_note-CDC_standing_order-41] It is composed of [nucleoside-modified mRNA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nucleoside-modified_messenger_RNA) (modRNA) that encodes a mutated form of the full-length [spike protein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coronavirus_spike_protein) of SARS-CoV-2, which is encapsulated in [lipid nanoparticles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solid_lipid_nanoparticle).[^cite_note-pmid33053279-42] Initial guidance recommended a two-dose regimen, given 21 days apart;[^cite_note-CDC_standing_order-41][^cite_note-NCT04368728-43][^cite_note-palca-44][^cite_note-herper-45] this interval was subsequently extended to up to 42 days in the United States,[^cite_note-CDC_standing_order-41][^cite_note-46] and up to four months in Canada.[^cite_note-47][^cite_note-48]

Clinical trials began in April 2020;[^cite_note-NCT04368728-43] by November 2020, the vaccine had met the primary efficacy goals of the [phase III clinical trial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phases_of_clinical_research#Phase_III), with over 40,000 people participating.[^cite_note-BioNTechUpdate-49] [Interim analysis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interim_analysis) of study data showed a potential [efficacy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vaccine_efficacy) of 91.3% in preventing [symptomatic infection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Symptoms_of_COVID-19) within seven days of a second dose and no serious safety concerns.[^cite_note-herper-45][^cite_note-\:4-50] Most [side effects](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Side_effect) are mild to moderate in severity and resolve within a few days.[^cite_note-Comirnaty_EPAR-2][^cite_note-\:7-51] Common side effects include mild to moderate pain at the injection site, [fatigue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fatigue), and [headaches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Headache).[^cite_note-pmid33301246-52][^cite_note-53] Reports of serious side effects, such as allergic reactions, remain very rare[^cite_note-AEfrequency-55] with no long-term complications documented.[^cite_note-Comirnaty_EPAR-2][^cite_note-nhs-cvac-56]

The vaccine is the first COVID‑19 vaccine to be authorized by a [stringent regulatory authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stringent_regulatory_authority) for emergency use[^cite_note-ukgov12-22-57][^cite_note-boseley2-58] and the first to be [approved](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Approved_drug) for regular use.[^cite_note-Swiss_authorization-34] In December 2020, the United Kingdom was the first country to authorize its use on an emergency basis.[^cite_note-nhs-cvac-56] It is authorized for use at some level in the majority of countries.[^cite_note-59][^cite_note-60][^cite_note-61][^cite_note-62] On 23 August 2021, the Pfizer–BioNTech vaccine became the first COVID-19 vaccine to be approved in the US by the [Food and Drug Administration](11632.md) (FDA).[^cite_note-FDA_Comirnaty-29][^cite_note-FDA_PR_20210823-37][^cite_note-CNN_20210823-63] The logistics of distributing and storing the vaccine present significant challenges due to the requirement for its storage at extremely low temperatures.[^cite_note-Rosales-2021-64]

In August 2022, a [bivalent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bivalent_vaccine) version of the vaccine (Pfizer-BioNTech COVID-19 Vaccine, Bivalent) was authorized for use as a [booster dose](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Booster_dose) in individuals aged twelve and older in the US.[^cite_note-FDA_PR_20220831-65][^cite_note-66][^cite_note-67][^cite_note-68] The following month, the BA.1 version of the bivalent vaccine (Comirnaty Original/Omicron BA.1 or tozinameran/riltozinameran)[^cite_note-69] was authorized as a booster for use in the UK.[^cite_note-MHRA_PR_20220903-70][^cite_note-71][^cite_note-ema010922-72] The same month, the European Union authorized both the BA.1 and the BA.4/BA.5 (tozinameran/famtozinameran) booster versions of the bivalent vaccine.[^cite_note-Comirnaty_EPAR-2][^cite_note-Comirnaty_Union_Register-3][^cite_note-EMA_PR_20220916-32] In August 2024, the FDA approved and granted emergency authorization for a monovalent Omicron KP.2 version of the Pfizer–BioNTech COVID-19 vaccine.[^cite_note-FDA_PR_20240822-73][^cite_note-74] The approval of Comirnaty (COVID-19 Vaccine, mRNA) (2024-2025 Formula) was granted to BioNTech Manufacturing GmbH.[^cite_note-FDA_PR_20240822-73] The EUA amendment for the Pfizer-BioNTech COVID-19 Vaccine (2024-2025 Formula) was issued to Pfizer Inc.[^cite_note-FDA_PR_20240822-73] The emergency use authorization was revoked in August 2025.[^cite_note-FDA_Pfizer-BioNTech_COVID-19_Vaccine-75]

## Medical uses

The Pfizer–BioNTech COVID-19 vaccine is used to provide protection against COVID-19, caused by infection with the [SARS-CoV-2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Severe_acute_respiratory_syndrome_coronavirus_2) virus, by eliciting an immune response to the S [antigen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antigen).[^cite_note-Comirnaty_SmPC-1][^cite_note-Comirnaty_EPAR-2][^cite_note-Pfizer-BioNTech_COVID-19_Vaccine_FDA_label-40][^cite_note-Comirnaty_FDA_label-30] The vaccine is used to reduce morbidity and mortality from COVID-19.[^cite_note-CDC_standing_order-41]

The vaccine is supplied in a multidose vial as "a white to off-white, sterile, preservative-free, frozen suspension for [intramuscular injection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intramuscular_injection)".[^cite_note-Pfizer-BioNTech_COVID-19_Vaccine_FDA_label-40][^cite_note-Comirnaty_FDA_label-30][^cite_note-FDA_EUA_Report-76] It must be thawed to room temperature and diluted with [normal saline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Normal_saline) before administration.[^cite_note-Comirnaty_FDA_label-30][^cite_note-FDA_EUA_Report-76]

The initial course consists of two doses.[^cite_note-CDC_standing_order-41][^cite_note-Comirnaty_FDA_label-30] The [World Health Organization](33583.md) (WHO) recommends an interval of three to four weeks between doses. Delaying the second dose by up to twelve weeks increases [immunogenicity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Immunogenicity), even in older adults, against all [variants of concern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Variant_of_concern).[^cite_note-pitch-8weeks-77] Authors of the Pitch study think that the optimal interval against the [Delta variant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SARS-CoV-2_Delta_variant) is around eight weeks, with longer intervals leaving receptors vulnerable between doses.[^cite_note-pitch-8weeks-77]

A third, fourth, or fifth dose can be added in some countries.[^cite_note-FDA_PR_20220831-65][^cite_note-FDA_PR_20210812-78][^cite_note-CDC_immunocompromised-79][^cite_note-EMA_extra_doses-80][^cite_note-81][^cite_note-82]

### Effectiveness

|  | This section **needs more [reliable medical references](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:Identifying_reliable_sources_\(medicine\)) for [verification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:Verifiability) or relies too heavily on [primary sources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:Primary_sources)**. Unsourced or poorly sourced material may be challenged and [removed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:Verifiability#Burden_of_evidence). *Find sources:* ["Pfizer–BioNTech COVID-19 vaccine"](https://www.google.com/search?as_eq=wikipedia\&q=%22Pfizer%E2%80%93BioNTech+COVID-19+vaccine%22) – [news](https://www.google.com/search?tbm=nws\&q=%22Pfizer%E2%80%93BioNTech+COVID-19+vaccine%22+-wikipedia\&tbs=ar\:1) **·** [newspapers](https://www.google.com/search?\&q=%22Pfizer%E2%80%93BioNTech+COVID-19+vaccine%22\&tbs=bkt\:s\&tbm=bks) **·** [books](https://www.google.com/search?tbs=bks\:1\&q=%22Pfizer%E2%80%93BioNTech+COVID-19+vaccine%22+-wikipedia) **·** [scholar](https://scholar.google.com/scholar?q=%22Pfizer%E2%80%93BioNTech+COVID-19+vaccine%22) **·** [JSTOR](https://www.jstor.org/action/doBasicSearch?Query=%22Pfizer%E2%80%93BioNTech+COVID-19+vaccine%22\&acc=on\&wc=on) *(November 2021)* |

&#x20;A test-negative[^cite_note-83] [case-control study](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Case-control_study) published in August 2021, found that two doses of the BNT162b2 (Pfizer) vaccine had 93.7% effectiveness against symptomatic disease caused by the alpha (B.1.1.7) variant and 88.0% effectiveness against symptomatic disease caused by the delta (B.1.617.2) variant.[^cite_note-LopezBernallAugust2021-84] Notably, effectiveness after one dose of the Pfizer vaccine was 48.7% against alpha and 30.7% against delta, similar to effectiveness provided by one dose of the [ChAdOx1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ChAdOx1) nCoV-19 vaccine.[^cite_note-85]

In August 2021, the US [Centers for Disease Control and Prevention](6811.md) (CDC) published a study reporting that the effectiveness against infection decreased from 91% (81–96%) to 66% (26–84%) when the [Delta variant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SARS-CoV-2_Delta_variant) became predominant in the US, which may be due to unmeasured and residual [confounding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confounding) related to a decline in vaccine effectiveness over time.[^cite_note-86]

Unless indicated otherwise, the following effectiveness ratings are indicative of clinical effectiveness two weeks after the second dose. A vaccine is generally considered effective if the estimate is ≥50% with a >30% lower limit of the 95% [confidence interval](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confidence_interval).[^cite_note-87] Effectiveness is generally expected to slowly decrease over time.[^cite_note-88]
